Authorities alleged that he brought his family to make the trip appear legitimate and conceal the smuggling operation.</p><p>In the second case, authorities prevented an attempt to smuggle a foreigner out of the country to help him evade restrictions and arrest orders issued against him.</p><p>Investigations pointed towards a woman from a Gulf country who allegedly planned and carried out the operation with her daughter's help. As a GCC national, she reportedly exploited the ease of movement and travelled to the UAE with family members to conceal her activities.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/I47PTAQKE5HCXJRTNV2VXHFYRA.jpg?auth=6b0c75cf795078b7f212cc37e35b92caa79f78d4c43937002a079748971f7269&smart=true&width=2100&height=1575" alt="All those involved in the two operations were arrested. Photo: Wam" height="1575" width="2100"/><p>Officials also allege that she repeatedly smuggled infiltrators out of the country, and said her son-in-law had previously been accused in similar cases.</p><p>Authorities carried out several arrests and the Public Prosecution is continuing to investigate the two cases.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/I47PTAQKE5HCXJRTNV2VXHFYRA.jpg?auth=6b0c75cf795078b7f212cc37e35b92caa79f78d4c43937002a079748971f7269&amp;smart=true&amp;width=2100&amp;height=1575" type="image/jpeg" height="1575" width="2100"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Several people accused of involvement in the two operations were arrested. Photo: Wam]]></media:description></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[UAE's €40 billion investment is a powerful gesture, says German Chancellor]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/11/uaes-40-billion-investment-is-a-powerful-gesture-says-german-chancellor-friedrich-merz/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/11/uaes-40-billion-investment-is-a-powerful-gesture-says-german-chancellor-friedrich-merz/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[The National]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 13:15:58 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>German Chancellor Friedrich Merz has said he is impressed by the scale of the UAE's investment in <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/opinion/editorial/2026/09/11/uae-germany-visit-middle-east-europe-politics-diplomacy-economy/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/opinion/editorial/2026/09/11/uae-germany-visit-middle-east-europe-politics-diplomacy-economy/">Germany</a>.</p><p>The €40 billion ($46.47 billion) was announced on Thursday during President Sheikh Mohamed's state visit to Berlin.</p><p>The money will be used across sectors including advanced technology, artificial intelligence, digital infrastructure and energy, with €10 billion to be invested in the region of Bavaria.</p><p>“I am impressed by the UAE’s willingness to invest in Germany. It is a powerful signal,” read a statement released on Friday from the office of Mr Merz.</p><p>“The investment will bring jobs, competitiveness and the strength to innovate. President Sheikh Mohamed impressed me with his expertise and commitment: he knows Germany well as a place to do business. </p><p>“The €40 billion shows that international investors have enormous trust in Germany as a place to invest and innovate. I thank the UAE for its trust.”</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/CKVVXCHKRQBW5UYEDTXPCZG7MI.jpg?auth=6ec318a600ccd62e9e5ed3e2c2c8f3f81cc9b7cc62077516b90353dd85e4ce2b&amp;smart=true&amp;width=5472&amp;height=3648" type="image/jpeg" height="3648" width="5472"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[President Sheikh Mohamed with German Chancellor Friedrich Merz. EPA]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">CHRISTIAN MARQUARDT / POOL</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Middle East Film and Comic Con opens in Abu Dhabi with stars, cosplay and a growing creative community]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2026/09/11/middle-east-film-and-comic-con-abu-dhabi-location-tickets-guests-timing-price/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2026/09/11/middle-east-film-and-comic-con-abu-dhabi-location-tickets-guests-timing-price/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Faisal Al Zaabi]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 13:14:26 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Abu Dhabi is filling with superheroes, manga artists, professional wrestlers, cosplayers and collectors as the 14th Middle East Film &amp; Comic Con begins.</p><p>The convention opened at Adnec Centre on Friday and runs until Sunday, bringing together celebrity guests, international comic book artists and regional creators alongside gaming tournaments, cosplay competitions, anime experiences and shelves of collectibles.</p><p>More than 46,000 people attended last year's event, and MEFCC has continued to expand from a convention built around comics and film into a broader celebration of popular culture.</p><p>Among the biggest draws this year is the celebrity line-up. WWE star and actor <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/pop-culture/2025/12/12/john-cena-last-wwe-match-the-ring-general-gunther/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/pop-culture/2025/12/12/john-cena-last-wwe-match-the-ring-general-gunther/">John Cena</a> is appearing alongside <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/03/10/why-one-piece-continues-to-resonate-worldwide-as-netflix-launches-season-two/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/film-tv/2026/03/10/why-one-piece-continues-to-resonate-worldwide-as-netflix-launches-season-two/"><i>One Piece</i></a> actors Mackenyu and Jacob Romero Gibson, <i>Guardians of the Galaxy</i> actress Pom Klementieff, <i>Avatar: The Last Airbender</i> star Arden Cho, <i>Bridgerton</i> actress Charithra Chandran and <i>Star Wars</i> actor Joonas Suotamo.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/YTB6LH6QVFFONN6KE3J3DJFTRY.jpg?auth=578205cd8e9f99e2bffea6164d767606223e6f3f7bc2e125f0f17d8e79295cf2&smart=true&width=4171&height=2781" alt="Mohamed Khalifa Al Mubarak, chairman of the Department of Culture and Tourism – Abu Dhabi, at MEFCC, which has returned to the capital for its 14th outing. Victor Besa / The National" height="2781" width="4171"/><p>Fans can attend panels and Q&amp;As on the Main Stage or purchase vouchers for photographs and autographs in the Meet the Stars area.</p><p>But much of what gives MEFCC its character is happening away from the main stage.</p><p>Rows of independent artists, illustrators and small businesses are offering everything from self-published manga to handmade phone cases and original artwork.</p><p>Omani digital comic artist Fatima Al Lawati has been attending MEFCC since 2017. She writes and illustrates her own manga, which she began developing in 2004 before publishing the first book in 2015.</p><p>Her stall also includes key chains, pins, stickers and posters based on her work.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/TRBIYOQAMVGOJGY5W2ITB6AIMU.jpg?auth=405db69565d4933a930ca4c4eab18e8c9580878939732c5752656ec6fc53a836&smart=true&width=4904&height=3269" alt="Omani digital comic artist Fatima Al Lawati showcases her self-published manga and other original work. Victor Besa / The National" height="3269" width="4904"/><p>“Every year it becomes more enjoyable. I get to meet new people, meet new artists and celebrities, and the event itself is so much fun,” she says of returning to Comic Con.</p><p>Duaa Alrefaie, the artist behind UAE-based Shades Studio, is attending her second MEFCC with products made using decoden, a Japanese decorative technique that often uses cream-like materials to create elaborate designs reminiscent of cake icing.</p><p>Her products range from customised phone cases and jewellery boxes to compact mirrors, photo holders and key chains.</p><p>“The people here, the community and the buyers, appreciate this form of art more than anywhere else I have exhibited in the UAE,” she says.</p><p>“I wanted to come back to present my work somewhere where it is appreciated.”</p><p>That local creative presence extends to Sandstorm Comics, the Abu Dhabi publisher developing original comics and children's books.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/LB6RULTQVFD6HLFNZPOVSNWJFQ.jpg?auth=d6af9b0eea71b33cafe4b7e3db83226b07b23f056e87699b669a5d4e5d3a7358&smart=true&width=5340&height=3560" alt="Duaa Alrefaie of Shades Studio creates phone cases, jewellery boxes and other accessories using the Japanese craft of decoden. Victor Besa / The National" height="3560" width="5340"/><p>This year, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/books/2025/04/20/solarblader-sandstorm-comics-mo-abedin-abu-dhabi/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/books/2025/04/20/solarblader-sandstorm-comics-mo-abedin-abu-dhabi/">Sandstorm</a> is bringing smaller editions of some of its comics, which adopt dimensions closer to those of manga, alongside its standard larger editions.</p><p>Editor-in-chief <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2026/08/17/inside-playgrounds-abu-dhabis-community-led-celebration-of-collector-culture/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2026/08/17/inside-playgrounds-abu-dhabis-community-led-celebration-of-collector-culture/">Mo Abedin</a> says research conducted by the company found its audience divided almost evenly between the two formats.</p><p>“Some people enjoy the art in a larger format because, obviously, it's a comic,” he says. “But some people also want something on the go, something they can put in their bags or take on a plane.”</p><p>Sandstorm is also placing greater emphasis on Little Storm, its children's publishing line built around Emirati identity, stories and values.</p><p>“Children finally have books they can read with people who look like them, eat the same food they eat and have the same names they have,” Abedin says.</p><p>The convention's collector culture is just as visible.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/JDVEAO5WAZCFTOFTJIDRH7HXYE.jpg?auth=a1e1cf8d6ea3e867d5bffca69aad742b188d8396ef8c24d911a73b58343d5cc0&smart=true&width=5404&height=3603" alt="Collectibles remain a major draw, with visitors able to browse figures, trading cards, comics and limited-edition merchandise across the convention floor. Victor Besa / The National" height="3603" width="5404"/><p>Retailers and exhibitors are offering figures, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/meet-the-funko-pop-collector-who-has-more-than-600-models-in-his-dubai-villa-1.64789" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts/meet-the-funko-pop-collector-who-has-more-than-600-models-in-his-dubai-villa-1.64789">Funko Pops</a>, trading cards, apparel, comics and other memorabilia, while MEFCC exclusives include limited-edition comics, designer toys, Sega clothing, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2024/11/02/sonic-the-hedgehog-naoto-ohshima/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2024/11/02/sonic-the-hedgehog-naoto-ohshima/"><i>Sonic the Hedgehog</i></a> plushies and trading cards.</p><p>The Comic Creators Club and Artist Alley are bringing visitors face to face with illustrators and comic artists, while Mohammed Bin Rashid Library is making its MEFCC debut with Arabic and English books, comics and manga. </p><p>Anime streaming platform Crunchyroll is among this year's new exhibitors and is hosting its Ultimate Anime Experience with licensed installations, photo opportunities and giveaways.</p><p>The Otaku District adds J-pop and J-rock covers, karaoke, dance performances, quizzes, choreography workshops and Random Play Dance.</p><p>Those who prefer competition can head towards the new TCG Arena. <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/money/2024/07/09/collecting-pokemon-cards-no-longer-childs-play-as-investment-returns-surge/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/money/2024/07/09/collecting-pokemon-cards-no-longer-childs-play-as-investment-returns-surge/"><i>Pokemon</i></a>, <i>One Piece</i> and <i>Magic: The Gathering</i> tournaments are running across the weekend, with 300 player places in total as well as sessions for newcomers.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/T6C5RYKGUNBGFIDXOYGEPX4FBQ.jpg?auth=bac0a6e88bf3f52758b9b6f5068d23e05c08a0b98db13f279bbd349a1cf3aedd&smart=true&width=5665&height=3777" alt="Cosplay is a major part of MEFCC, with fans dressing as characters from across pop culture. Victor Besa / The National" height="3777" width="5665"/><p>Cosplay remains one of MEFCC's most visible traditions. This year's competition has a Dh60,000 prize pool, while the winning UAE team in the World Cosplay Summit preliminary round will earn the opportunity to represent the country at the 2027 finals in Nagoya, Japan.</p><p>Visitors who prefer to participate without competing can join the Cosplay Catwalk or meet international cosplayers, including Aokiji and Marmalade.</p><p>A <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2026/03/11/how-an-egyptian-gaming-studio-helps-bring-resident-evil-requiems-characters-to-life/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/arts-culture/2026/03/11/how-an-egyptian-gaming-studio-helps-bring-resident-evil-requiems-characters-to-life/"><i>Resident Evil</i></a> installation recreates a house from the upcoming film, filling it with interactive details including cryptic telephone recordings and a typewriter that references the video games.</p><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/things-to-do/2026/09/07/harry-potter-land-abu-dhabi-warner-bros-world/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/lifestyle/things-to-do/2026/09/07/harry-potter-land-abu-dhabi-warner-bros-world/">Warner Bros World Abu Dhabi</a> is bringing a Batmobile photo opportunity and a DC costume competition, while Black+Decker has a collaborative sketch wall, DIY shelf-building activities and artists customising air fryers live during the weekend.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/47WDNQPCQRDTHGWNU46CLYQDX4.jpg?auth=0169ee8c74c5ef664633a1cdcae803eb7fc33ddfc5ede4e02e6797222b6ac075&smart=true&width=5586&height=3724" alt="Tenxia Anime Dioramas is among the exhibitors selling detailed pop culture collectibles. Victor Besa / The National" height="3724" width="5586"/><p>The Bookworm Lounge, Collectors Gallery and Tabletop Zone offer alternatives to the crowds around the stages, while the Festival Plaza brings together carnival games and food trucks, including Salt, Wing Stop, Moshi and House of Pops.</p><p>Some visitors have arrived hoping for a photograph with Cena. Others are spending hours looking through comic boxes, competing in a card tournament, examining an independent artist's prints or showing off costumes that have taken months to build.</p><p>For the artists returning year after year, however, the attraction can be considerably simpler.</p><p>Asked what she loves most about Comic Con, Alrefaie doesn't mention the celebrities or merchandise, but says simply: “The community.”</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/VGFYWLLGOREL5KDHJ47EMYRONE.jpg?auth=6102f2d11b1b5d41f5f6c0493df8d462befd2065d51ce04db18aabf44105a88c&amp;smart=true&amp;width=5480&amp;height=3653" type="image/jpeg" height="3653" width="5480"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[MEFCC brings together independent retailers, artists and collectors alongside its celebrity panels and pop culture attractions at Adnec Centre. Victor Besa / The National]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">VB</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Supply chain disruptions make UAE's €40 billion investment in Germany crucial, says minister]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/11/supply-chain-disruptions-make-uaes-40-billion-investment-in-germany-crucial-says-minister/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/11/supply-chain-disruptions-make-uaes-40-billion-investment-in-germany-crucial-says-minister/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Shireena Al Nowais]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 13:09:50 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Recent disruptions to global supply chains were a major factor in the UAE's decision to invest €40 billion ($46.47 billion) in <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/09/president-sheikh-mohamed-bin-zayed-arrives-in-germany-to-begin-state-visit/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/09/president-sheikh-mohamed-bin-zayed-arrives-in-germany-to-begin-state-visit/">Germany</a>, said Dr Thani Al Zeyoudi, Minister of Foreign Trade.</p><p>The investment was announced on Thursday evening, during President Sheikh Mohamed's state visit to Germany.</p><p>The money will be invested across a range of sectors including industry, advanced technology, artificial intelligence, digital infrastructure and energy, with €10 billion of the investment to be used specifically in Bavaria.</p><p>"The relationship between the UAE and Germany is very strategic,” said Dr Al Zeyoudi. “We are talking about two complementary countries that can take each other forward.</p><p>“What has been happening around the world in recent years, and particularly in recent months, has disrupted supply chains, energy supplies and technological advancement. Technology is also disrupting the way conventional sectors operate.”</p><p>In the Middle East region alone, there have been repeated disruptions to <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/2026/07/17/war-risk-shipping-premium-surges-again-as-tensions-escalate-at-strait-of-hormuz/" target="_blank" rel="">commercial shipping</a> in the Strait of Hormuz since the outbreak of the Iran war in late February, the reach of which has extended far beyond the GCC.</p><p>It is critical for the UAE to continue strengthening international partnerships, with Germany one of its most important economic partners in Europe, said Dr Al Zeyoudi.</p><p>“Germany is an economic powerhouse and an industrial hub, as well as a centre for talent and technology,” he said. “This comes at the right time, taking all these factors into consideration and ensuring that we move the relationship forward.”</p><p>Dr Al Zeyoudi said some of the projects covered by the agreements were close to implementation and their effects would be felt immediately, although others would require more time. </p><p>“You will see some of the results immediately,” he said. “Some of the projects are already about to be implemented.”</p><p>He identified energy, technology and logistics as the three most important areas for future investment. </p><p>“Energy comes first because it has historically been one of the main sectors in our bilateral relationship,” he said. “The second is technology and data centres, where there is huge potential, and the third is logistics."</p><blockquote class="twitter-tweet"><p lang="en" dir="ltr">UAE President and German Chancellor witness launch of Strategic Dialogue, declaration of intent to establish UAE-German Investment Council, and other agreements<a href="https://x.com/hashtag/WamNews?src=hash&amp;ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">#WamNews</a> <a href="https://t.co/rC0yd2AxJ7">https://t.co/rC0yd2AxJ7</a> <a href="https://t.co/DTTFoAEbme">pic.twitter.com/DTTFoAEbme</a></p>&mdash; WAM English (@WAMNEWS_ENG) <a href="https://x.com/WAMNEWS_ENG/status/2098284779955519561?ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">September 11, 2026</a></blockquote><h2><b>Mutual benefits</b></h2><p>Dr Al Zeyoudi said Germany would benefit from the UAE’s position as a global logistics and trade hub, its investment capital and its access to markets in the Middle East, Asia and Africa.</p><p>The UAE, in turn, would gain from German industrial knowledge and technology.</p><p>“Germany will benefit from our logistics and trade capabilities, the capital we have and the market access we can provide to many German industries and businesses,” he said.</p><p>“We are elevating this relationship, doubling down on our historical investments in Germany and strengthening our trade relationship. At the same time, we are ensuring the right frameworks are in place for our businesses to move quickly.”</p><p>He said the investments should not be viewed as conventional financial transactions but as long-term partnerships between Emirati and German businesses.</p><p>“People will see more German technology being applied and used in the UAE,” he said. “We will bring more investment from our side into Germany and establish more partnerships.</p><p>“Some of the projects will be implemented using technology from Germany, and some operations will also be based on that technology, and vice versa. We do not see this as conventional investment. It is a partnership between both sides.”</p><h2><b>High expectations</b></h2><p>A new UAE-Germany Investment Council will be responsible for following up on the agreements and helping to ensure that major projects move from announcements to implementation.</p><p>“The council has to ensure there is a framework for following up on these projects and that the major projects are implemented,” Dr Al Zeyoudi said. “Some of its members are leading projects themselves on behalf of their institutions, so expectations of them are very high.”</p><p>Asked whether one side stood to benefit more than the other, he said the test of the partnership would be the value it created for people in both countries and across their respective regions.</p><p>“What matters is that it benefits people,” he said. “It does not matter if one party benefits in one area more than the other, as long as it takes the relationship to the next level and creates value for our people, their people and the people of our regions.”</p><p>Further details of the investment projects will be announced as feasibility work is completed, he said.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/4LMCAXHMDJCPZEMO62FPEBGSGE.jpg?auth=652091638998c74e02ba9b684353f26cd98faa7f04025d95cfe9789928438db1&amp;smart=true&amp;width=4200&amp;height=2801" type="image/jpeg" height="2801" width="4200"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[German Chancellor Friedrich Merz and Dr Thani Al Zeyoudi, Minister of Foreign Trade, in Berlin. Ryan Carter / UAE Presidential Court
]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">Ryan Carter / UAE Presidential Court</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[The Editor's Briefing]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/newsletters/the-editors-briefing/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/newsletters/the-editors-briefing/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Mina Al-Oraibi]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 13:09:06 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><style>

<!--view online start-->
<table border="0" cellpadding="0" cellspacing="0" class="deviceWidth view-online-link hidden_lg hidden_sm hidden_md" style="min-width:600px;padding-bottom:8px" width="600">
<tbody><tr valign="top">
<td align="center"><a class="view-online-link" style="text-decoration:none; font-size: 16px" href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/newsletters/the-editors-briefing/">View online</a></td>
</tr>
</tbody></table></p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/VVUGLBCLW5EHPLMK4Q7N45L5W4.jpg?auth=54c2d37805bfa936a4fd93b2d0b58e80c265576f5ecf22ffa82f8723f01b9342&smart=true&width=2222&height=500" alt="" height="500" width="2222"/><p>As the summer lull fades, daily life across much of the Gulf feels deceptively “normal”. Yet news of occasional drone attacks and dramatic Houthi attacks on Saudi Arabia in the past week serve as a sharp reminder that the normal of “no peace, no war” is unacceptable to all sides. Even with a nominal ceasefire in place, Tehran and its network of proxies continue targeting regional states and critical waterways, while the US regularly strikes back.</p><p>Amid low-level diplomatic efforts, officials are increasingly focused on ways to restore deterrence. While a short-term peace deal is essential to halt strikes and reopen international waterways, long-term deterrence will be what actually keeps the peace. Most US and Arab diplomats acknowledge that the Iranian regime, despite serious domestic troubles, is here to stay for now – and with the cheap drone technology and <a href="https://are01.safelinks.protection.outlook.com/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thenationalnews.com%2Fnews%2Fmena%2F2026%2F09%2F11%2Fhow-yemeni-rebels-used-anthropics-ai-software-to-design-and-build-guided-weapons%2F&amp;data=05%7C02%7CDCheriyan%40thenationalnews.com%7Ce106f2add6e54686dc6408df1004de04%7Ce52b6fadc5234ad692ce73ed77e9b253%7C0%7C0%7C639247285509463071%7CUnknown%7CTWFpbGZsb3d8eyJFbXB0eU1hcGkiOnRydWUsIlYiOiIwLjAuMDAwMCIsIlAiOiJXaW4zMiIsIkFOIjoiTWFpbCIsIldUIjoyfQ%3D%3D%7C0%7C%7C%7C&amp;sdata=%2FtVvuGxmssVrijatmxjoH0GLZYa%2BbwSCRwzQUYmJhHI%3D&amp;reserved=0" target="_blank" rel="">accessibility to AI</a>, it retains the capacity to wreak havoc.</p><p>Listening to Qatar’s foreign ministry spokesman <a href="https://are01.safelinks.protection.outlook.com/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thenationalnews.com%2Fnews%2Fgulf%2F2026%2F09%2F07%2Fqatar-insists-strait-of-hormuz-will-remain-vital-to-global-economy-even-as-alternative-routes-emerge%2F&amp;data=05%7C02%7CDCheriyan%40thenationalnews.com%7Ce106f2add6e54686dc6408df1004de04%7Ce52b6fadc5234ad692ce73ed77e9b253%7C0%7C0%7C639247285509481769%7CUnknown%7CTWFpbGZsb3d8eyJFbXB0eU1hcGkiOnRydWUsIlYiOiIwLjAuMDAwMCIsIlAiOiJXaW4zMiIsIkFOIjoiTWFpbCIsIldUIjoyfQ%3D%3D%7C0%7C%7C%7C&amp;sdata=zvcaiBqLV1hFc6WF%2BWd9b0qNjedRYlwTFmtWhK8fa2s%3D&amp;reserved=0" target="_blank" rel="">Majed Alansari</a> speak in Abu Dhabi this week, I was struck by his emphasis on “restoring deterrence” as the primary challenge, and his warning that accepting the current status quo as a "new normal" is the greatest threat. Dr Anwar Gargash, diplomatic adviser to the UAE President, echoed this sentiment, noting that while diplomatic relations with Iran may be restored, "restoring trust is the mountain we must climb." In the absence of that trust, deterrence becomes all the more vital. As events become more dramatic around the work, I wonder if we can bring back deterrence - and by extension restraint and decorum. </p><p>If restoring deterrence represents diplomacy at its most difficult and defensive, events in Germany this week, meanwhile, showcased diplomacy at its most ambitious. UAE President Sheikh Mohamed undertook the first state visit by an Emirati leader to Germany, and elevated relations between the countries with agreements across sectors. Ceremonial displays were backed by major economic deals - including a huge €40 billion investment package targeting advanced technology, energy and digital infrastructure. Yet, as Sheikh Mohamed posted on X, the visit was a reminder of “the importance of advancing sustainable peace in the region and around the world”.</p><p>As the world marks 25 years since the September 11 attacks, prompting reflections on the fallout of "forever wars," asymmetrical proxy conflicts, and the erosion of international law, "sustainable peace" remains the bold ambition we should all demand.</p><p><table style="width: 100%;padding: 10px 0 30px 0 !important;">
  <tr>
    <td style="width: 105px;     padding: 15px 0 0 0 !important; text-align: left;">
      <img src="https://thenational-the-national-sandbox.cdn.arcpublishing.com/resizer/v2/F3XNEE5O2NDADHX2CSKZK3K7FQ.png?auth=d703d8357152eeef62cc08a09f488c003b6161f4d594c40a4dd2f27d0f2fd8fc&public=false" width="105" />
    </td>
</tr>
<tr style="font-family: Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif;">
    <td style="vertical-align: middle;padding: 0 0 30px !important">
      <strong><i>Mina Al-Oraibi</i></strong><br><i>Editor-in-Chief</li>
    </td>

isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2026/09/11/dubai-will-come-back-atm-2026-focuses-on-tourism-recovery-backed-by-events-and-repeat-visitors/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Deena Kamel]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 13:00:00 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Dubai's travel and tourism officials are cautiously optimistic about a <a href="https://are01.safelinks.protection.outlook.com/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thenationalnews.com%2Fnews%2Fuae%2F2026%2F07%2F21%2Fdubai-offers-dh3000-in-hotel-and-leisure-deals-for-residents-inviting-family-and-friends-to-visit%2F&amp;data=05%7C02%7Csessaid%40thenationalnews.com%7C2c338829be8643e179ea08df1002ed0f%7Ce52b6fadc5234ad692ce73ed77e9b253%7C0%7C0%7C639247276918276491%7CUnknown%7CTWFpbGZsb3d8eyJFbXB0eU1hcGkiOnRydWUsIlYiOiIwLjAuMDAwMCIsIlAiOiJXaW4zMiIsIkFOIjoiTWFpbCIsIldUIjoyfQ%3D%3D%7C0%7C%7C%7C&amp;sdata=W%2FPMSlVmzC0uBcCCI%2FpVP6tchAwWmQVCsqGasqczAAg%3D&amp;reserved=0" target="_blank" rel="" title="Original URL: https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/07/21/dubai-offers-dh3000-in-hotel-and-leisure-deals-for-residents-inviting-family-and-friends-to-visit/. Click or tap if you trust this link.">rebound in the industry</a> despite the Iran war, betting on Emirates airline's rapid recovery, <a href="https://are01.safelinks.protection.outlook.com/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.thenationalnews.com%2Fnews%2Fuae%2F2026%2F06%2F21%2Ftourists-eager-to-return-to-dubai-after-travel-warnings-scrapped%2F&amp;data=05%7C02%7Csessaid%40thenationalnews.com%7C2c338829be8643e179ea08df1002ed0f%7Ce52b6fadc5234ad692ce73ed77e9b253%7C0%7C0%7C639247276918302924%7CUnknown%7CTWFpbGZsb3d8eyJFbXB0eU1hcGkiOnRydWUsIlYiOiIwLjAuMDAwMCIsIlAiOiJXaW4zMiIsIkFOIjoiTWFpbCIsIldUIjoyfQ%3D%3D%7C0%7C%7C%7C&amp;sdata=HtQsllP6WbbmefWZYLNWAHT2zbXraSFrUM680QbZPTg%3D&amp;reserved=0" target="_blank" rel="" title="Original URL: https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/06/21/tourists-eager-to-return-to-dubai-after-travel-warnings-scrapped/. Click or tap if you trust this link.">a busy events calendar</a>, the appeal of the city's winter season, and close collaboration between the public and private sectors. </p><p>The city is preparing to host the Arabian Travel Market (ATM) from September 14 to 17 when the sector's recovery strategy will come into sharp focus against a backdrop of regional travel disruptions.</p><p>Emirates recorded “quite decent” passenger demand during the summer with the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/aviation/2026/06/09/emirates-airbus-a380-no-plans-to-cut-capacity-iran-war-tim-clark/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/aviation/2026/06/09/emirates-airbus-a380-no-plans-to-cut-capacity-iran-war-tim-clark/">airline restoring most of its pre-conflict capacity</a> and route network, despite region-wide travel disruptions and uncertainty during the ongoing conflict, Adnan Kazim, Emirates airline's deputy president, said at a press conference ahead of ATM.</p><p>The airline carried 8.7 million passengers in July and August, up from 4.7 million in March and April, he said. That is on a par with the number of passengers carried last summer, according to Emirates, which does not post monthly traffic figures.</p><p>The Dubai-based carrier restored 93 per cent of its capacity and 98 per cent of its route network compared to prewar levels, according to Mr Kazim. It now flies to 140 destinations in 72 countries, with a new route to Helsinki launching on October 1.</p><p>A <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/01/business-as-usual-uae-gears-up-for-september-reset-despite-spectre-of-iran-war/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/01/business-as-usual-uae-gears-up-for-september-reset-despite-spectre-of-iran-war/">collective focused effort on promoting Dubai</a> as a destination that is open for business is under way to instil confidence and comfort among travellers to the emirate.</p><p>“Dubai will come back and ATM will be a good milestone for that comeback to happen,” Mr Kazim said.</p><p>Underpinning this recovery are Dubai's popular winter season, a busy events calendar with more than 100 exhibitions in the last four months of this year, and repeat visitors to the city including the visiting friends and relatives (VFR) segment, he said.</p><p>The 33rd ATM will take place at Dubai World Trade Centre, with more than 80 destination participants and 300 host buyers. The event was postponed from its originally scheduled date in May due to the Iran war that has now entered its seventh month.</p><p>The annual event will bring together tourism ministers, authorities, hotels, tour operators and exhibitors to assess the conflict's impact, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2026/04/06/uae-preparing-support-package-for-tourism-sector-says-economy-minister/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2026/04/06/uae-preparing-support-package-for-tourism-sector-says-economy-minister/">rebuild traveller confidence</a> and discuss the sector's future.</p><p>Dubai attracted a record 19.6 million visitors in 2025, with highs of two million visitors in December alone, and the “trajectory is moving in the right direction”, said Issam Kazim, chief executive of Dubai Corporation for Tourism and Commerce Marketing. The current situation is affecting the number of visitors to the city because of some governments issuing travel advisories and some international airlines suspending flights, he added.</p><p>Dubai is working closely with stakeholders at local and federal levels, airlines, and other partners to restore flights to the destination quickly, Mr Kazim of DCTCM said.</p><p>“All the international relationships are being leveraged to ensure that … we get in as many carriers and opening flights as quickly as possible,” he said, adding that Emirates and flydubai are “plugging in the gaps” to ensure travel continues.</p><p>Despite the travel advisories and some international flight suspensions, Dubai's tourism recovery plan will not be limited to regional travel, he added. “We will always obviously be optimistic about international markets as well. [Foreign visitors] are the biggest advocates of the city.”</p><p>Dubai is tapping its expatriate population to help attract more international visitors and bolster its image as a safe destination open for business. It has launched a tourism incentive delivering up to <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/07/21/dubai-offers-dh3000-in-hotel-and-leisure-deals-for-residents-inviting-family-and-friends-to-visit/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/07/21/dubai-offers-dh3000-in-hotel-and-leisure-deals-for-residents-inviting-family-and-friends-to-visit/">Dh3,000 ($816) in discounts to residents</a> who invite friends and family to visit the emirate.</p><p>Haitham Mattar, regional director of IHG Hotels and Resorts, said it has been a “challenging time” for the tourism industry, but the sector is “way to big to fail”.</p><p>“There is short-term impact on demand … however, there is continued investor confidence in markets like UAE, Saudi Arabia and Egypt,” he said.</p><h2><b>Long-term growth</b></h2><p>Dubai's tourism strategy is focused on long-term growth rather than short-term volatility.</p><p>“Whether it was the financial crisis or Covid or anything else, Dubai always is looking forward,” Mr Mattar said. “We don't plan by quarters, we plan for decades ahead.</p><p>“We want to make sure that as soon as things are back to normal, we're ready to pick up in a better stance and at a much stronger pace,” he added. “What better point to start than ATM itself?”</p><h2><b>'A different ATM'</b></h2><p>Danielle Curtis, regional portfolio director at RX Global that organises ATM, said this year ATM will be “different”, but no less important.</p><p>“There has been a little concern from international destinations coming in; however, our message is very clear: Dubai is open and it's business as usual,” she told <i>The National</i>. “It's important now more than ever for ATM to operate, to connect the industry.”</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/YQLJ5L3OCFFQXPBWCVCQDVL3PM.jpg?auth=77ebbdeb4e3889ad03aa6ddc9a34b511e08f2bdab4187b3f61c3074d0a45a2dd&amp;smart=true&amp;width=8192&amp;height=5464" type="image/jpeg" height="5464" width="8192"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Dubai's tourism strategy is focused on long-term growth rather than short-term volatility. Seen here, tourists at the city's Spice Souq. Victor Besa / The National]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">VICTOR BESA</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[News quiz of the week: September 11, 2026]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/2026/09/11/news-quiz-of-the-week-september-11-2026/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/2026/09/11/news-quiz-of-the-week-september-11-2026/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[The National]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 12:57:28 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><style>

Baniyas Cemetery serves as a landmark on this stretch when driving towards Dubai.</p><p>Drivers are advised to reduce their speed and comply with the strict limit to avoid penalties, which range from Dh300 to Dh3,000. </p><p>Abu Dhabi enforces a strict zero-tolerance policy for speeding, with radars activating as soon as a vehicle exceeds the limit by 1kph, unlike Dubai and Sharjah, which allow a 20kph buffer.</p><p>If a motorist exceeds the speed limit by more than 80kph, they will also receive 23 black points and the vehicle will be impounded for 60 days. Releasing an impounded vehicle may incur additional fees of up to Dh50,000.</p><p>Separately, the Integrated Transport Centre has announced the temporary closure of two left lanes on Sheikh Maktoum bin Rashid Road (E11) towards Al Shahama, from 12am on Sunday until 5am on Monday.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/DZX6SOZOBJG7DEWYGPCQW3X3NQ.jpg?auth=cf147b10b326fb199f20c32de3a2e1fc4bf58cee6dbbbd7cd5665c7662a37ac2&amp;smart=true&amp;width=1280&amp;height=960" type="image/jpeg" height="960" width="1280"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[The speed limit will be lowered from 120kph to 100kph on the E311 between Tawi Al Qaymah Street and Al Khayr Street in both directions. Photo: Ajanta Paul]]></media:description></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Abu Dhabi to Berlin flights to launch on October 15 under Etihad partnership]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/11/abu-dhabi-to-berlin-flights-to-launch-on-october-15-under-etihad-partnership/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uae/2026/09/11/abu-dhabi-to-berlin-flights-to-launch-on-october-15-under-etihad-partnership/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Shireena Al Nowais]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 12:04:31 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Direct flights between Abu Dhabi and Berlin will be launched on October 15 under an agreement between Etihad and the German airline Condor.</p><p>The start date of the new route – which was previously announced in December – was confirmed in Munich on Friday. </p><p>Condor planes will be used to transport passengers to and from Zayed International Airport to Berlin's Brandenburg Airport.</p><p>Dr Nadia Bastaki, Etihad’s chief people, government and corporate affairs officer, said the start of the route was a significant step.</p><p>“We are really proud to be here today because this marks a very important milestone for the UAE and Germany,” she said. “As we heard today, there are significant partnerships happening across all sectors, especially in transport and aviation.”</p><p>The route was unveiled a day after Germany announced it would open access to Berlin Brandenburg Airport to UAE airlines.</p><h2>Landmark state visit</h2><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/VQ2SLXBLUJFFXHKPT436J26JRU.jpg?auth=3e11eadb908b9209de2fe13847b35ca316bb4d789822db46a69e9fea7ca58af2&smart=true&width=4200&height=2801" alt="President Sheikh Mohamed meets former chancellor Angela Merkel as part of his state visit to Germany. honour guards at the reception for Sheikh Mohamed at Villa Borsig" height="2800" width="4200"/><p>It was one of a raft of announcements made on Thursday as part of President Sheikh Mohamed's state visit to Germany.</p><p>Etihad’s presence in Germany stretches back 22 years, with the airline launching its first service to the country in 2004. The Abu Dhabi carrier now operates 35 flights a week to destinations in Germany. Its partnership with Condor is expected to add between 12 and 14 weekly services in different phases.</p><p>“Our first flight with Condor will start on October 1 from Berlin to Abu Dhabi,” Dr Bastaki said. “That marks a really important partnership. Condor will be able to use Abu Dhabi as a destination, but also use our network of more than 100 destinations, enabling people to fly from Germany to the east or elsewhere.”</p><p>Condor's partnership with Etihad also includes flights between Frankfurt and Abu Dhabi and connections through the UAE capital to destinations including Bangkok.</p><p>Dr Bastaki said strong demand on Etihad’s German routes supported the case for further expansion. The airline’s current services are operating at a load factor of about 90 per cent, meaning nine out of every 10 available seats are occupied.</p><p>“Our flights today are operating at a 90 per cent load factor,” she said. “That means the demand is there and people want to travel and use Etihad as part of their journey.”</p><p>She said the new services would give passengers more choice and help build stronger links between the two countries. “There is a direct market between Berlin and Abu Dhabi, but it also helps to build bridges,” she said.</p><p>Asked about Etihad’s longer-term plans for Germany, she said the airline wanted to increase frequencies at the four points covered by the bilateral air services agreement.</p><p>“We want to increase frequency across all four points in the bilateral,” she said. “If that allows us to go into different cities in Germany, that will also be part of our future plans.”</p><p>Germany is one of Etihad’s most important European markets and the airline expects stronger air links to support growing commercial and tourism ties. About 2,000 German companies have offices in the UAE, with many using the country as a base from which to serve markets across the Middle East, Africa and Asia.</p><p>“Germany is very important for us within the European sector,” Dr Bastaki said. “It is one of the main markets, not only for the airline but also for Abu Dhabi. The capitals need to be connected, and that is why the national airline, together with other airlines coming into Abu Dhabi and the UAE, can help support that partnership.”</p><p>Dr Bastaki said Etihad was also investing in efforts to address aviation’s environmental impact. “Carbon footprint is on the agenda of every airline,” she said. “We are invested in it, we offset our carbon footprint and we recognise that everybody in the aviation sector has a responsibility.”</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/EQKYGHMHEREXPKJW73LL4QBF3I.jpg?auth=a696855e1da2be853ffaa8435a691bdd817383079a335e6d2102e2073ff0245d&amp;smart=true&amp;width=5184&amp;height=3456" type="image/jpeg" height="3456" width="5184"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Etihad Airways has joined forces with German airline Condor to launch the flights. Getty Images]]></media:description><media:credit role="author" scheme="urn:ebu">NurPhoto</media:credit></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Guardians of the energy markets gather in Bangkok]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/11/guardians-of-the-energy-markets-gather-in-bangkok/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/11/guardians-of-the-energy-markets-gather-in-bangkok/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Manus Cranny]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 12:00:00 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>Sir <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/05/26/europe-must-forge-strong-alliance-with-middle-east-urges-tony-blair/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/05/26/europe-must-forge-strong-alliance-with-middle-east-urges-tony-blair/">Tony Blair</a> knows what happens when energy, politics and the Middle East collide.</p><p>He entered Downing Street in 1997 with oil below $20 a barrel and left a decade later with it averaging around $70-$75 – three consecutive election wins bracketing a nearly fourfold rise in the price of energy. The morning he resigned, he became the Quartet’s special envoy to the Middle East, a post he held until 2015. </p><p>That makes the former British prime minister a particularly interesting figure to have in Bangkok this week, where he will join me on stage at the Gastech Gala Dinner.</p><p>Because this is no ordinary gathering of the energy industry.</p><p>“This war will end immediately after our election.” That was President Donald Trump on Wednesday, tying the end of the Iran conflict to the US midterms on November 3 – a remark that came the same day Brent crude broke back above $100 a barrel for the first time in six weeks.</p><p>Sources close to the White House have said petrol prices won’t fall before the vote either. Washington, in the meantime, keeps tightening the screw: a new sanctions package hit Iran’s aviation sector on Tuesday, days after the Treasury moved to dismantle what it called the regime’s remaining revenue streams.</p><h2><b>Nobody thought we’d be here</b></h2><p>The guardians of the energy industry gather in Bangkok this week at ground zero for the economic and social fallout from parabolic moves in oil, gas and fertiliser.</p><p>And it was gas that put a stranglehold on these economies, knocking Pakistan into a four-day working week, forcing Thailand to ration power and suspend fuel exports, and pushing China to ban its own refiners from exporting diesel, petrol and jet fuel.</p><p>As we convene, Asian gas prices have ratcheted up nearly 15 per cent in the past month and have more than doubled over the year.</p><p>That’s the backdrop: a tight market, no end in sight and a room full of people who actually have to answer for it. Few are better placed to do that than the panel I’ll sit down with on Monday – the heads of gas at ExxonMobil, Shell, Eni, Adnoc and Chevron.</p><h2><b>Milan to Bangkok</b></h2><p>Twelve months ago in Milan, the mood was dominance. Chris Wright, the US Secretary of Energy, called net zero a “colossal train wreck”, with Doug Burgum, Secretary of the Interior, beside him selling American liquefied natural gas (LNG) as the fix for Europe.</p><p>Bangkok is a different register.</p><p>And Blair is a rare thing at a conference like this – a politician who lived this exact swing in real time and then spent the following decade dealing with its geopolitics.</p><p>His Institute for Global Change argued last year that phasing out fossil fuels in the short term was “doomed to fail”, a case that reads less like contrarianism now and more like description.</p><p>The questions for him at Gastech: what does resolution actually look like from here, and what would it mean for global energy markets when it comes?</p><p>Fatih Birol, executive director of the International Energy Agency, has called this crisis a “major, major threat” to the global economy. Haitham Al Ghais, Opec’s Secretary General, insists it’s a “one-off event ”.</p><p>Both can’t be right. Bangkok is where that gets tested against the data.</p><h2><b>'Long and variable lag'</b></h2><p>Milton Friedman, US economist and statistician, gave central bankers the phrase. Usually it describes monetary policy. It works just as well for energy right now: the delayed reckoning for a war that hasn’t fully landed yet, more than six months on.</p><p>European gas storage is at a two-decade low, while TTF, Europe's primary natural gas pricing benchmark, is up more than 130 per cent this year. JPMorgan sees the next leg landing in fertiliser, with global food inflation set to climb from 2.8 per cent to around 5 per cent by early 2027.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/OCIO75XH6BHSHD6VQJQE4GKO24.jpg?auth=8590ad8b3422f2bc21539930427c2e57963674a55b382c5d09a7c5384380bf1a&smart=true&width=1000&height=800" alt="" height="800" width="1000"/><p>That’s the real subject of Gastech 2026 – not the crisis behind the industry, but the one still working its way downstream.</p><p>I’ve covered this market as a banker, a broker and a television anchor, three decades watching Opec ministers and oil chief executives move it with a single sentence, from three different sides of the desk.</p><p>What isn’t familiar this time is a chokepoint itself becoming the headline.</p><p>Gastech was last in Asia in 2008. It returns to a region that has spent this war rationing fuel, not just talking about its price.</p><h2><b>'The vase is broken'</b></h2><p>The Strait of Hormuz carries almost one fifth of the world’s LNG, nearly all of it Qatari and Emirati. When Iran shut it, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2026/09/10/qatar-and-oman-pitch-middle-east-backed-financing-for-infrastructure-projects-in-global-south/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/economy/2026/09/10/qatar-and-oman-pitch-middle-east-backed-financing-for-infrastructure-projects-in-global-south/">Qatar’s exports</a> collapsed by about 96 per cent – an estimated $24 billion in lost sales, with Ras Laffan repairs alone projected to cost up to $20 billion a year for as long as five years.</p><p>Mr Birol put the deeper problem to this publication in July: the vase is broken.</p><p>Saudi Arabia and the UAE have clawed back close to 85 per cent of prewar export levels, but recovered volume isn’t recovered confidence. The IEA doesn’t see a full supply recovery before 2027.</p><p>The strait was a fact of life nobody had to test until this war did. Iran and Oman are now negotiating what amounts to a tollbooth, with Iran floating $1 million-$2 million a vessel and Oman countering with a “voluntary” fee structure.</p><p>The free-navigation assumption behind 60 years of energy trade is being renegotiated in real time.</p><p>That reframes Bangkok’s central question, and it’s the one I intend to put to the heads of gas this week: if the strait moves again – a second closure, a re-escalation, call it what you like – what happens to the market this time, now that buyers have seen the price of being caught out once?</p><p>Shell can lean on <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/2026/09/07/winter-is-coming-the-lng-crisis-europe-cannot-afford-to-ignore/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/2026/09/07/winter-is-coming-the-lng-crisis-europe-cannot-afford-to-ignore/">LNG Canada</a> to redirect cargoes to Asia. Chevron has Gorgon and Wheatstone in Australia. Eni has Mozambique. ExxonMobil sits on the other side of that table, exposed through Qatar and the UAE while its new Golden Pass supply out of Texas heads mostly towards Europe. Adnoc, meanwhile, has spent months building pipeline and east-coast capacity that looks, in hindsight, like insurance.</p><p>The question isn’t only who has spare barrels. It’s who was exposed and who wasn’t – and who gets paid for having supply in the right place when Asia needs it most.</p><p>Emerging Asia queued for fuel, rationed gas and absorbed a price shock it did nothing to cause.</p><p>Eighteen years after Bangkok last hosted <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/markets/2025/09/08/manus-on-markets-opec-oil-output-hike-set-to-dominate-gastech-discussion/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/markets/2025/09/08/manus-on-markets-opec-oil-output-hike-set-to-dominate-gastech-discussion/">Gastech</a>, the guardians of the energy markets are gathering here again, in the region that paid for a war it didn’t start – and with Washington now saying, in as many words, that the war doesn’t end before the ballots are counted.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/X65OWH4WZZHZTEXPRDPRGYCPTM.jpg?auth=70a805488bd01a1487384fbcf91c0959a55c3b7e6e06832452597a7a03d902a9&amp;smart=true&amp;width=6452&amp;height=4303" type="image/jpeg" height="4303" width="6452"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Sir Tony Blair will be appearing on stage at the Gastech Gala Dinner in Bangkok. The rest of Britain thinks so ]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/09/11/is-london-crime-ridden-the-rest-of-britain-thinks-so/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/09/11/is-london-crime-ridden-the-rest-of-britain-thinks-so/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Tariq Tahir]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 11:53:11 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>London is seen by the rest of the UK as being a city rife with crime, despite statistics showing a decline in incidents, a new report has found.</p><p>King's College London used polling of the wider population and concluded that people overestimate crime in the UK capital.</p><p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/london/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/london/">London</a> has in recent years acquired a <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/01/23/london-travel-crime-reputation-mobile-phone-theft-rolex-robbers/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/01/23/london-travel-crime-reputation-mobile-phone-theft-rolex-robbers/">reputation </a>for dangerous streets, driven by <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/06/24/london-to-use-crime-fighting-drones-to-tackle-phone-thefts-tarnishing-citys-image/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/06/24/london-to-use-crime-fighting-drones-to-tackle-phone-thefts-tarnishing-citys-image/">thefts of mobile phones</a>, which has dented its image as a safe place to visit and work.</p><p>This has partly been <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2025/09/23/sharia-law-in-london-trump-tells-unga-thats-what-migrants-intend-for-uk/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2025/09/23/sharia-law-in-london-trump-tells-unga-thats-what-migrants-intend-for-uk/">fuelled </a>by <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/donald-trump/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/donald-trump/">Donald Trump’s</a> feud with the city’s mayor, <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/07/16/london-mayor-sadiq-khan-granted-seat-in-the-house-of-lords/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/07/16/london-mayor-sadiq-khan-granted-seat-in-the-house-of-lords/">Sadiq Khan.</a> The US President claimed there were parts of the capital where police were “afraid to go” because there was “no law and order”.</p><p>Mr Khan has pushed back, with his office releasing <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/crime/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/crime/">crime</a> statistics that show London has a murder rate lower than comparable European cities and well below those in the US. The Metropolitan Police has also stepped up operations against street crime, including those carried out by <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/09/10/scores-of-e-bike-bandits-arrested-in-london-crackdown-on-ride-by-robberies/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/09/10/scores-of-e-bike-bandits-arrested-in-london-crackdown-on-ride-by-robberies/">robbers on e-bikes</a>.</p><blockquote><p>London has become a lightning rod in our national debate</p><p class="citation">Prof Bobby Duffy</p></blockquote><p>But the report, <i>London has fallen? Misperceptions of crime, race, religion and life in the UK capital</i>, has found that many people in the UK may share Mr Trump’s view of the city.</p><p>The polling, carried out by YouGov, found that 62 per cent guessed that total crime in London had risen between 2019 and 2025, when it actually fell by 1.4 per cent.</p><p>Much of London’s reputation has been driven by videos on social media. </p><p>Half of the public believe London had the highest rate of violent crime of any region in the year to September 2025, when the city had the lowest rate.</p><p>They also have an inflated view of the risk of crime in London, guessing 40 per cent of Londoners were victims of crime in a single year, more than three times the reality of 12.2 per cent.</p><h2><b>London life</b></h2><p>Londoners appear to have a different view of their own city, including on its ethnic make-up. </p><p>The UK public believes the <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/muslim/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/muslim/">Mus</a><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/muslim/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/tags/muslim/">lim</a> share of London’s population is 35 per cent, when the reality is 15 per cent. The figure rises to a half among supporters of the right-wing populist Reform UK party.</p><p>A third of the UK public as a whole believed there were areas where Sharia dominates, and non-Muslims cannot enter. In London, 18 per cent believed they exist while two-thirds of Londoners rejected the idea.</p><p>Sharia is a complex, expansive body of Islamic law that guides Muslims' daily lives, providing direction on issues including worship and relationships.</p><p>But there are some things the UK public gets right, with 55 per cent correctly believing that London was the worst UK region for theft from a person.</p><p>The report points out that London’s reputation in the UK is at odds with it topping the World’s Best Cities Index in 2025, run by the consultancy Resonance.</p><p>Addressing the report’s findings, Mr Khan said the “gulf between the real London and the false idea of London that exists inside some people’s heads has never been more pronounced”.</p><p>“The cause is a dark blizzard of disinformation about our capital city online, where London is described as a lawless dystopia in decline,” said Mr Khan, writing in <i>The Times</i>.</p><p>“None of this is to say London is perfect. Like every big city, we have our challenges and I’ll always defend people’s right to criticise my policies. </p><p>“But you can acknowledge that London has forever been a work in progress while also giving short shrift to shameless efforts to trash our reputation.”</p><p>The report’s author, Professor Bobby Duffy, director of the Policy Institute at King’s College London, said that views of London already fit into “the story we already believe” about the city.</p><p>“London has become a lightning rod in our national debate, deeply connected to arguments about crime, immigration and the direction of the country, with the sense that ‘London has fallen’ now a recurring theme in political, media and social media narratives,” he said.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/ZRHY56KKFZBHHFB5FPOGIKMIOM.jpg?auth=5b12279beab3067bbe9b264bb6e5c7cf7687f3d285dd71b0e698913dc8d66da5&amp;smart=true&amp;width=5123&amp;height=3415" type="image/jpeg" height="3415" width="5123"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Police officers on Oxford Street in London. Owners will be told by next autumn whether their homes are deemed to be above the £2 million mark at which the charge is introduced.</p><p>HM Revenue and Customs has then built in a period to allow for appeals before the system goes live in spring 2028.</p><p>Owners who dispute the valuation of their home will be given time to check and challenge the charge, which was introduced by former chancellor <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2025/08/19/rachel-reeves-puts-londons-property-market-in-her-pre-budget-crosshairs/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2025/08/19/rachel-reeves-puts-londons-property-market-in-her-pre-budget-crosshairs/">Rachel Reeves</a> in last year’s budget.</p><p>Owners would be expected to pay £2,500 a year for <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/property/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/property/">properties</a> worth £2 million to £2.5 million, and £3,500 for properties worth up to £3.5 million.</p><p>Properties between £3.5 million and £5 million would incur a charge of £5,000 annually, while those worth more than £5 million would face a £7,500 levy. The surcharge, officially named the high value council tax surcharge, is separate from the council tax and would rise annually with inflation. </p><p>It is expected to affect around 200,000 homes or 1 per cent of properties, mostly in London and the south-east of the country. There have been complaints that the £2 million threshold in London is too low, as it could easily apply to a terraced house rather than a mansion.</p><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/TXI2Q33GPBFBNNO2OCBR2GORP4.jpg?auth=4725fd7c4c02cf642f89d84ebb05715845a79ac702b9ef2ef60875880a4d9c91&smart=true&width=4000&height=3274" alt="An aerial view of houses in Holland Park, London. Getty Images" height="3274" width="4000"/><p>John-Paul Marks, the chief executive of HMRC, told the Treasury Committee this week that 300 extra staff would be needed within the Valuation Office to implement the tax.</p><p>Committee chairman Meg Hillier said there was a “lot of worry out there that people will have inspectors visiting their home, checking their extension” for valuations.</p><p>Mr Marks said valuations would be done using AI and publicly available data such as property sales and registry, with <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/08/24/uk-launches-home-inspections-to-determine-mansion-tax-wealth-surcharge/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/uk/2026/08/24/uk-launches-home-inspections-to-determine-mansion-tax-wealth-surcharge/">inspections in some cases</a>, usually at the request of a homeowner who wished to challenge a decision. </p><p>Visits would be a last resort, and only with the agreement of the homeowner, he said. Home visits currently take place in disputes over council tax banding. </p><p>“Want to make valuations a confident, trusted process that people can engage with and see the methodology and data sources that they’re drawn on,” Mr Marks said, with the intention that would reduce the number of appeals.</p><p>“The list would be published in advance, so that customers would have a chance to check it, to challenge it if for any reason they think it’s not accurate and ultimately appeal if there was a dispute.”</p><p>The Valuation Office has previously said inspections would be required where “attributes can only be confirmed internally or a re-measurement is required”.</p><p>Anyone who “intentionally delays or obstructs” a valuation officer carrying out their duties faces a £200 fine, ministers said last month. Failing to provide information without a “reasonable excuse” could result in a fine of up to £500.</p><p>James Cleverly, the Conservatives’ shadow housing secretary, said families faced tax inspectors “snooping around their gardens and inside their homes, to justify hiking taxes on them”.</p><p>The government has said the tax was “addressing a long-standing unfairness, where a Band D home in Darlington pays more in council tax than a £10 million mansion in Mayfair”. </p><p>It said: “The Valuation Office has extensive experience valuing domestic property, and will use a wide range of evidence to determine bandings.”</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/23OXW6R2LRHGPKM4ID5LBC7OW4.jpg?auth=eec8878ff6f2d70f72539dba068f420d5ac77b7fae18d3787d619c561b2ffd16&amp;smart=true&amp;width=4000&amp;height=2670" type="image/jpeg" height="2670" width="4000"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Homes in London's Notting Hill. Photo: Etihad]]></media:description></media:content></item><item><title><![CDATA[Oil prices set to end the week above $100 as Houthis seize key port city]]></title><link>https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/11/oil-prices-set-to-end-the-week-above-100-as-houthis-seize-key-port-city/</link><guid isPermaLink="true">https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/11/oil-prices-set-to-end-the-week-above-100-as-houthis-seize-key-port-city/</guid><dc:creator><![CDATA[Fareed Rahman]]></dc:creator><description></description><pubDate>Fri, 11 Sep 2026 10:07:55 +0000</pubDate><content:encoded><![CDATA[<p><a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/10/oil-prices-hit-105-per-barrel-as-houthis-seize-key-red-sea-port/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/business/energy/2026/09/10/oil-prices-hit-105-per-barrel-as-houthis-seize-key-red-sea-port/">Oil prices</a> traded sideways on Friday and were on track to end the week above $100 per barrel for the first time in nearly four months as supply concerns from the Middle East rose after <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/10/live-iran-us-war-houthis-yemen/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/news/mena/2026/09/10/live-iran-us-war-houthis-yemen/">Houthi rebels</a> seized a key port city along the Red Sea and ships continue to come under attack in the Strait of Hormuz.</p><p>Brent, the benchmark for two-thirds of the world's oil, was down 2.31 per cent to $105 a barrel at 11.16am UAE time. West Texas Intermediate, the gauge that tracks US crude, was trading 1.96 per cent lower at $100.5 a barrel.</p><p>Oil prices edged higher early in the morning after touching $109 per barrel in the previous session. Oil, however, pared gains later in the day.</p><p>“Prices jumped as Houthi forces advanced towards coastal areas near the strategic Bab Al Mandeb Strait, including a push towards Mokha, raising fears of deeper disruption to Red Sea shipping,” Soojin Kim, a Dubai-based analyst at Japanese lender MUFG, said.</p><p>“The escalation compounds existing pressure on Gulf supplies, with Houthi attacks earlier in the week forcing some Saudi energy facilities to halt operations while US-Iran fighting continues to threaten tanker traffic through Hormuz.”</p><p>Houthi rebels on Thursday seized Mokha, a key port city along the Red Sea near Bab Al Mandeb Strait that is critical for Saudi Arabia’s oil flow to Asian markets. </p><p>The new development has raised fears that the Yemen-based group could increase its influence along the strait and disrupt oil exports that could further tighten oil markets and put upward pressure on prices.</p><h2><b>Ship traffic hits another low</b></h2><p>Preliminary data from Kpler shows ship traffic across the Bab Al Mandeb strait reduced on Thursday, with 27 vessels crossing the key chokepoint for global crude, down from 32 the previous day. Of the total, 16 exited the Red Sea, while 11 entered. </p><p>A Russia-flagged vessel exited the sea carrying about 791,000 barrels of crude towards the Indian port of Sikka. Vessels were also going towards Singapore, Bangladesh, China and Indonesia.</p><p>Strait of Hormuz ship traffic also remains thin as the US and Iran engage in tit-for-tat attacks on oil tankers.</p><p>Earlier this week, the US military destroyed five Iranian oil tankers, while Iran said it retaliated by attacking 10 ships near the key channel.</p><p>Only seven vessels crossed the Strait of Hormuz on Thursday, down from 12 on Wednesday and 18 on Tuesday, according to Kpler. More than 100 vessels used to transit the route, carrying 20 per cent of global crude and liquefied natural gas supplies, before the conflict in February. </p><p>“The key question for oil markets is no longer whether Brent can break above $100. It already has,” said Priyanka Sachdeva, an analyst at Phillip Nova. “The question now is whether the market can stabilise below $120, or whether another wave of supply disruption pushes crude into a completely new price regime.”</p><p>Global benchmark Brent crude touched an intraday high of $126.41 per barrel on April 30, marking a four-year high as a result of the Iran war and its impact on supplies.</p><p>Saudi production has also fallen sharply amid constraints on both its Gulf and Red Sea export routes, which is supporting oil prices.</p>]]></content:encoded><media:content url="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/2RSH4QLKUZE4HHYWK547XCZOCU.jpg?auth=c6ad84eca767a9873be361fe372c9395c7e8fa7f2df495c43c9fd73abada9fa6&amp;smart=true&amp;width=4200&amp;height=2800" type="image/jpeg" height="2800" width="4200"><media:description type="plain"><![CDATA[Ship traffic through the Strait of Hormuz continues to remain thin amid renewed US-Iran strikes. Expect red flags, crashes and a lot of chaos as drivers attempt to navigate 22 unfamiliar corners. </p><p>Our power rankings measure who will arrive in Spain in the best shape. We take into account recent form, raw speed, how much each driver is extracting from their car, momentum, and how well their strengths might suit Madrid. </p><p>After Zandvoort and Monza, here are our top 10 as well as our prediction for the race in Spain. </p><blockquote class="twitter-tweet"><p lang="en" dir="ltr">The centrepiece of the MADRING: La Monumental 🤩 <a href="https://x.com/hashtag/F1?src=hash&amp;ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">#F1</a> <a href="https://x.com/hashtag/SpanishGP?src=hash&amp;ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">#SpanishGP</a> <a href="https://t.co/cF9Ki6xK5s">pic.twitter.com/cF9Ki6xK5s</a></p>&mdash; Formula 1 (@F1) <a href="https://x.com/F1/status/2097735535041171744?ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">September 9, 2026</a></blockquote><h2><b>10 Nico Hulkenberg (Audi)</b></h2><p><b>Form: </b>Zandvoort P8, Monza P12.<b> Championship: </b>P15<b> </b></p><p>Hulkenberg continues to squeeze plenty of pace out of the Audi. He started 13th in Zandvoort and climbed to eighth, picking up four useful points along the way. At Monza, he finished 12th, but his recent performances keep highlighting the same qualities. His experience means he reads races well and capitalises on opportunities. </p><h2><b>9 Arvid Lindblad (Racing Bulls) </b></h2><p><b>Form: </b>Zandvoort P12, Monza P8. <b>Championship: </b>P11<b> </b></p><p>Lindblad’s 12th place at Zandvoort was overshadowed by Max Verstappen’s crash. A drive-through penalty for passing Pierre Gasly under double yellow flags dropped him out of contention after he was forced to take evasive action. Lindblad called the punishment “quite extreme” and felt eighth place had been within reach. </p><p>At Monza, Lindblad reached Q3 for a sixth successive weekend and raced from 10th to eighth, splitting the Alpines despite Racing Bulls losing speed on the straights. He has now scored in seven of the past eight races. For a 19-year-old rookie, he’s forcing us to pay attention. Madrid will prove tricky, and crashes are to be expected. Lindblad may suffer. </p><h2><b>8 Charles Leclerc (Ferrari) </b></h2><p><b>Form: </b>Zandvoort P5, Monza DNF.<b> Championship: </b>P5<b> </b></p><p>If Antonelli has been dubbed an ‘AI Agent’ for his ability to adapt and improve with each race, then Leclerc is a mere human, flaws and all. He had plenty of pace across both weekends but crashing out in Monza because he was too aggressive on the chicane against Lewis Hamilton is typical Leclerc. Many drivers make mistakes, and yet we always remember Leclerc’s. </p><p>He finished second in the Zandvoort sprint and fifth in the Grand Prix due to the virtual safety car – where he was perhaps a little unlucky as he had enough pace to push for a podium. Madrid offers him the opportunity to regroup. </p><h2><b>7 Oscar Piastri (McLaren)</b></h2><p><b>Form: </b>Zandvoort P6, Monza P5. <b>Championship: </b>P7<b> </b></p><p>Piastri started strongly at Zandvoort and passed Russell after the restart, but his pace faded dramatically after his first stint, leaving him perturbed. A slow pit stop compounded the problem and both Ferraris eventually passed him, leaving his team principal to conclude that the performance deserved better. </p><p>Monza produced a slightly improved result. He qualified third before a grid penalty dropped him to sixth, but he finished fifth after Norris passed him late on. There is clearly more pace in Piastri and the McLaren, but over the past two weekends Norris has been struggling to extract more from the car. </p><blockquote class="twitter-tweet"><p lang="en" dir="ltr">Not the pole conversion Gasly wanted, but a brilliant race nonetheless 👏 <a href="https://x.com/hashtag/F1?src=hash&amp;ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">#F1</a> <a href="https://x.com/hashtag/ItalianGP?src=hash&amp;ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">#ItalianGP</a> <a href="https://t.co/XLyh1m2WfO">pic.twitter.com/XLyh1m2WfO</a></p>&mdash; Formula 1 (@F1) <a href="https://x.com/F1/status/2097773787064475671?ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">September 9, 2026</a></blockquote><h2><b>6 Pierre Gasly (Alpine)</b></h2><p><b>Form: </b>Zandvoort P10, Monza P7<b> Championship: </b>P10<b> </b></p><p>Considering his performance in Monza, perhaps Gasly deserves to be even higher in the rankings. He produced a superb final lap in qualifying to claim <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2026/09/05/pierre-gasly-clinches-special-maiden-pole-position-at-italian-gp/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2026/09/05/pierre-gasly-clinches-special-maiden-pole-position-at-italian-gp/">the first pole position of his F1 career</a>, six years after claiming his <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/pierre-gasly-wins-crazy-italian-grand-prix-after-lewis-hamilton-is-hit-by-penalty-1.1073786" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/pierre-gasly-wins-crazy-italian-grand-prix-after-lewis-hamilton-is-hit-by-penalty-1.1073786">maiden victory</a> at the same circuit. </p><p>His driving, coupled with the team’s performance in helping him to capitalise from the powerful slipstream, allowed him to make history. A point at Zandvoort and a P7 finish in Monza demonstrate positive steps. Alpine can push for bigger targets in Madrid. </p><h2><b>5 Lewis Hamilton (Ferrari) </b></h2><p><b>Form: </b>Zandvoort P4, Monza P6<b> Championship: </b>P3<b> </b></p><p>Hamilton has made his frustrations with Ferrari clear. At Zandvoort, he felt a podium was within reach. Running on fresher tyres, Hamilton was stuck behind Leclerc while Ferrari debated how to manage the two cars. His sarcastic message, “Great waste of time there, guys”, summed up his mood. </p><p>At Monza, Hamilton started fourth but dropped towards 10th after Leclerc squeezed him into the gravel as they fought through the opening chicane. He recovered to finish sixth. Fred Vasseur, Ferrari’s team principal, has promised better from the team in Madrid. </p><h2><b>4 George Russell (Mercedes)</b></h2><p><b>Form: </b>Zandvoort P3, Monza P2<b> Championship: </b>P2<b> </b></p><p>“It’s embarrassing, isn’t it? To start at the front and be beaten by your teammate from the back,” said David Coulthard on the <i>Up to Speed</i> podcast. Despite good drives at nearly all the circuits, Russell is suffering, as he sees his young teammate extract excellence from the same car to win consistently. </p><p>It makes it worse that Russell started in P2 at Monza. Russell has been superb in his own right over the past two weekends, but his inability to adapt his driving to secure maximum speed has been concerning. </p><blockquote class="twitter-tweet"><p lang="en" dir="ltr">Welcome to Max&#39;s Monza overtaking masterclass 🤩 <a href="https://x.com/hashtag/F1?src=hash&amp;ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">#F1</a> <a href="https://x.com/hashtag/ItalianGP?src=hash&amp;ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">#ItalianGP</a> <a href="https://t.co/xZrrZiYTkC">pic.twitter.com/xZrrZiYTkC</a></p>&mdash; Formula 1 (@F1) <a href="https://x.com/F1/status/2097717667511857573?ref_src=twsrc%5Etfw">September 9, 2026</a></blockquote><h2><b>3 Max Verstappen (Red Bull)</b></h2><p><b>Form: </b>Zandvoort DNF, Monza P3<b> Championship:</b> P6 </p><p>It’s controversial to place Verstappen so high on this list, considering his home weekend ended on the opening lap, but his response at Monza was emphatic. From fifth on the grid, he worked his way into the lead and kept enough pressure on Mercedes to influence their strategy before finishing on the podium. “I tried to battle, but we were just too slow on the straight,” said Verstappen. </p><p>The Dutchman's experience should make the difference in Madrid as the grid attempts to navigate a new chaotic circuit. </p><h2><b>2 Lando Norris (McLaren) </b></h2><p><b>Form: </b>Zandvoort P1, Monza P4<b> Championship: </b>P4<b> </b></p><p>Norris was outstanding at Zandvoort. He <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/2026/08/22/lando-norris-produces-best-lap-of-my-life-to-seal-dutch-gp-pole-with-title-race-leader-kimi-antonelli-third/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/2026/08/22/lando-norris-produces-best-lap-of-my-life-to-seal-dutch-gp-pole-with-title-race-leader-kimi-antonelli-third/">took pole in qualifying</a>, lost the lead to Antonelli after the restart, but then he fought back past the championship leader and drove away to make it <a href="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2026/08/23/lando-norris-seals-back-to-back-wins-with-victory-in-dutch-grand-prix/" target="_blank" rel="" title="https://www.thenationalnews.com/sport/f1/2026/08/23/lando-norris-seals-back-to-back-wins-with-victory-in-dutch-grand-prix/">back-to-back wins</a>. Monza was always going to be a difficult circuit for the car due to McLaren’s weakness in straight-line speed and yet Norris climbed from eighth to fourth and beat his teammate again. </p><p>He’s certainly one of the grid’s most in-form drivers and will feel confident of the car in Madrid, a circuit better suited to its strengths. </p><h3><b>Antonelli wins Italian GP – in pictures</b></h3><img src="https://www.thenationalnews.com/resizer/v2/PJ4XNPZQDRCADC6QWQQHS4UBYE?auth=4e7454a51e9ce908352357385123a6b48a88414d44cb67d656bb2fc826e61d88&smart=true&width=2835&height=1595" alt="Mercedes driver Kimi Antonelli celebrates his historic win at the 2026 Italian Grand Prix at Monza.
